% 11283: equal to $2n - 2\alpha - \mu = \mu$ (notation of (2.1.9), where by
% 11284: hypothesis $\lambda = 0$), which is also that of $\Tl(A_K)^t$. As the
% 11285: inclusion
% 11286: \[
% 11287: \Tl(A_K)^t \subset \Tl(A_K)
% 11288: \]
% 11289: is \emph{strict}, in the sense that it already induces a monomorphism
% 11290: for all terms of the projective systems under consideration, it follows
% 11291: easily that the first member of (*) is maximal among the
% 11292: sub-pro-Barsotti--Tate groups of $\Tl(A_K)$ having the same rank as it,
% 11293: so that one indeed has the equality of (5.2.7), which is equivalent to
% 11294: the inclusion between the extreme terms of 5.2.7 by the orthogonality
% 11295: theorem.
% 11296: 
% 11297: \medskip
% 11298: 
% 11299: \noindent\textbf{5.3.} Using the correspondence by orthogonality between
% 11300: strict sub-pro-Barsotti--Tate groups (in the precise sense explained
% 11301: above) of the dual pro-groups $\Tl(A_K)$ and $\Tl(A'_K)$, one deduces
% 11302: formally from (5.2.2) that the pairing (1.0.2) between these pro-groups
% 11303: induces a pairing
% 11304: \begin{equation}
% 11305: \Tl(A_K)^{\ab} \times \Tl(A'_K)^{\ab} \longrightarrow
% 11306: \Tl(\GG_{mk})
% 11307: \end{equation}
% 11308: which is a \emph{perfect duality}, i.e. identifying each of the two
% 11309: factors of the first member with the dual of the other (in the sense of
% 11310: 5.2.2). (NB the exponents $\ab$ denote the ``abelian parts'', quotient
% 11311: of the fixed part by the toric part). It is clear moreover that (5.3.2)
% 11312: is induced by the pairing
% 11313: \begin{equation}
% 11314: \Tl(A^0)^{\ab} \times \Tl(A'^0)^{\ab} \longrightarrow
% 11315: \Tl(\GG_{mS})
% 11316: \end{equation}
% 11317: deduced by passage to the quotients from the pairing (5.2.4.2). This
% 11318: being a perfect duality on the generic fibres, i.e. the corresponding
% 11319: homomorphism
% 11320: \begin{equation}
% 11321: \Tl(A^0)^{\ab} \longrightarrow D(\Tl(A'^0)^{\ab})
% 11322: \end{equation}
% 11323: being an isomorphism on the generic fibres, we obviously want to deduce
% 11324: from this that it is the same over all of $S$, or equivalently, over
% 11325: the special fibres. This deduction is valid, trivially, if $\ell \neq p$;
% 11326: it is also valid, at least in the case of unequal characteristics, if
% 11327: $\ell = p$, thanks to a deep theorem of TATE [33, th.~4]. We shall
% 11328: however be able to establish this \emph{directly}, without the help of
% 11329: Tate's theorem, below (7.4.6).
% 11330: 
% 11331: To make precise the geometric meaning of the result we desire to
% 11332: establish, note that the pairing deduced from (5.3.3) by passage to the
% 11333: special fibres is none other than the pairing
% 11334: \begin{equation}
% 11335: \Tl(B_0) \times \Tl(B'_0) \longrightarrow \Tl(\GG_{mk})
% 11336: \end{equation}
% 11337: deduced from the biextension $W_0$ of $(A_0^0, A_0'^0)$ by passage to
% 11338: the quotient, where $B_0$ and $B'_0$ denote respectively the abelian
% 11339: parts of $A_0^0$ and $A_0'^0$. As, by virtue of VIII 4.8, the
% 11340: biextension $W_0$ comes from a biextension $W_0^{\ab}$ of $(B_0, B'_0)$
% 11341: by $\GG_{mk}$, determined up to unique isomorphism, the pairing (5.3.5)
% 11342: can also be interpreted as deduced from the divisor correspondence
% 11343: $W_0^{\ab}$, and the corresponding homomorphism
% 11344: \begin{equation}
% 11345: \Tl(B_0) \longrightarrow D(\Tl(B'_0)) \overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}
% 11346: \Tl(D(B'_0))
% 11347: \end{equation}
% 11348: (where in the last term, the letter $D$ denotes the dual abelian scheme)
% 11349: is none other than that deduced by functoriality of $\Tl$ from the
% 11350: homomorphism
% 11351: \begin{equation}
% 11352: B_0 \longrightarrow D(B'_0)
% 11353: \end{equation}
% 11354: defined by $W_0^{\ab}$. Hence the fact that (5.3.5) is a perfect duality
% 11355: also means, by the well-known dictionary (cf.~[5]) that the kernel of
% 11356: (5.3.7), which is a finite group over $k$ by virtue of 1.5, has a null
% 11357: $\ell$-primary component. Saying that this is so for all $\ell$ therefore
% 11358: means that the isogeny (5.3.7) is an \emph{isomorphism}, i.e. that the
% 11359: divisor correspondence $W_0^{\ab}$ is a perfect duality between $B_0$ and
% 11360: $B'_0$. The preceding considerations only show that the kernel of (5.3.7)
% 11361: is a $p$-group. Let us therefore state the more complete result :
% 11362: 
% 11363: \begin{theoreme}[5.4]
% 11364: Let $S$ be a trait, and $A_K$ an abelian scheme over $S$ having
% 11365: semi-stable reduction over $S$. Denote by $B_0$, $B'_0$ the abelian
% 11366: parts of the N\'eron models of $A_K$ and of the dual abelian scheme
% 11367: $A'_K$ respectively. Then the canonical divisor correspondence
% 11368: $W_0^{\ab}$ on $(B_0, B'_0)$, deduced by VIII~4.8 from the canonical
% 11369: biextension $W_0$ of $(A_0^0, A_0'^0)$ (special fibre of the biextension
% 11370: $W$ of 1.4), is a perfect duality between $B_0$ and $B'_0$, i.e. defines
% 11371: an isomorphism of each of these abelian schemes with the dual of the
% 11372: other.
% 11373: \end{theoreme}
% 11374: 
% 11375: The proof of 5.4 in the general case will be given in No.~7, as a
% 11376: consequence of a very natural and important construction due to RAYNAUD.
% 11377: Let us only note here that to prove 5.4, one may assume $S$ complete, in
% 11378: view of 3.3.
% 11379: 
% 11380: \medskip
% 11381: 
% 11382: \noindent\textbf{5.5.} To express conveniently the extreme factors in
% 11383: the filtrations (5.1.10) and (5.1.11), in the case of semi-stable
% 11384: reduction, let us introduce the twisted constant group schemes over $S$
% 11385: \begin{equation}
% 11386: \underline{M}' = D(T_0) \; , \quad \underline{M} = D(T'_0) \; ,
% 11387: \end{equation}
% 11388: duals of the maximal tori $T_0$, $T'_0$ of the special fibres of $A, A'$
% 11389: (SGA 3 X 5.7). As $S$ is local complete, these group schemes may be
% 11390: considered as the special fibres of twisted constant group schemes
% 11391: \begin{equation}
% 11392: \underline{M}' \; , \quad \underline{M} \quad \text{on } S \; ;
% 11393: \end{equation}
% 11394: thus, if the torus $T_0$ is split (for example if $k$ is separably
% 11395: closed) i.e. if $\underline{M}'_0$ is the constant group defined by the
% 11396: ordinary group
% 11397: \begin{equation}
% 11398: M' = \Hom_{k\text{-gr}}(T_0, \GG_{mk}) \; ,
% 11399: \end{equation}
% 11400: then $\underline{M}'$ is the constant group defined by $M'$ :
% 11401: \[
% 11402: \underline{M}' = \underline{M}'_S \; .
% 11403: \]
% 11404: One may also note that, as $T_0$ and $T'_0$ are isogenous (2.2.6), $T'_0$
% 11405: is split if $T_0$ is, so that there is reason to also introduce in this
% 11406: case
% 11407: \begin{equation}
% 11408: M = \Hom_{k\text{-gr}}(T'_0, \GG_{mk}) \; ,
% 11409: \end{equation}
% 11410: and one will then have
% 11411: \[
% 11412: \underline{M} = \underline{M}_S \; .
% 11413: \]
% 11414: This being posited, one will evidently have, for every integer $n$,
% 11415: isomorphisms
% 11416: \begin{equation}
% 11417: \underline{M}'_n \simeq D(T_n) \; , \quad \underline{M}_n \simeq
% 11418: D(T'_n) \; ,
% 11419: \end{equation}
% 11420: where $T'_n$ is defined in terms of $A'$ as $T_n$ in terms of $A$
% 11421: (5.1.2), whence one deduces canonical isomorphisms (where one sets
% 11422: $\ZZ_{\ell}(1) = \Tl(\GG_{mS})$) :
% 11423: \begin{equation}
% 11424: \Tl(T_n) \simeq \check{\underline{M}}'_n \otimes_{\ZZ} \ZZ_{\ell}(1)
% 11425: \; , \quad \Tl(T'_n) \simeq \check{\underline{M}}_n \otimes_{\ZZ}
% 11426: \ZZ_{\ell}(1) \; ,
% 11427: \end{equation}
% 11428: and by passage to the limit on $n$ canonical isomorphisms :
% 11429: \begin{equation}
% 11430: \begin{cases}
% 11431: \Tl(A^0)^t = \Tl(\hat{T}) \simeq \check{\underline{M}}' \otimes
% 11432: \ZZ_{\ell}(1) \\[1ex]
% 11433: \Tl(A'^0)^t = \Tl(\hat{T}') \simeq \check{\underline{M}} \otimes
% 11434: \ZZ_{\ell}(1) \; .
% 11435: \end{cases}
% 11436: \end{equation}
% 11437: In the semi-stable reduction case, one concludes by duality, using the
% 11438: orthogonality theorem 5.2 and relation (5.2.2), canonical isomorphisms :
% 11439: \begin{equation}
% 11440: \begin{cases}
% 11441: \Tl(A_K)/\Tl(A_K)^f \simeq \underline{M} \otimes_{\ZZ} \ZZ_{\ell}
% 11442: \\[1ex]
% 11443: \Tl(A'_K)/\Tl(A'_K)^f \simeq \underline{M}' \otimes_{\ZZ} \ZZ_{\ell}
% 11444: \; .
% 11445: \end{cases}
% 11446: \end{equation}
% 11447: One finds likewise, thanks to the same formula (5.2.2), canonical
% 11448: isomorphisms :
% 11449: \begin{equation}
% 11450: \begin{cases}
% 11451: \Tl(A_K)/\Tl(A_K)^t \simeq D(\Tl(A'_K)^f) \\[1ex]
% 11452: \Tl(A'_K)/\Tl(A'_K)^t \simeq D(\Tl(A_K)^f) \; ,
% 11453: \end{cases}
% 11454: \end{equation}
% 11455: where one will recall that the groups $\Tl(A_K)^f$ and $\Tl(A'_K)^f$
% 11456: appearing in the second member are the restrictions to $\eta$ of
% 11457: Barsotti--Tate pro-$\ell$-groups defined over $S$, $\Tl(A^0)^f$ and
% 11458: $\Tl(A'^0)^f$ respectively; the letter $D$ denotes here the duals in
% 11459: the sense of Barsotti--Tate pro-groups, namely $\Hom(-, \ZZ_{\ell}(1))$.
% 11460: 
% 11461: \begin{proposition}[5.6]
% 11462: Suppose $S$ complete and that $A_K$ has semi-stable reduction over $S$.
% 11463: Then for every prime number $\ell$ (which may be equal to the residual
% 11464: characteristic of $S$), the pro-$\ell$-Barsotti--Tate group
% 11465: $\Tl(A_K)^t$ is the generic fibre of a Barsotti--Tate group over $S$,
% 11466: of ``toric type'', given by (5.5.7), while $\Tl(A_K)/\Tl(A_K)^f$ is the
% 11467: generic fibre of a Barsotti--Tate group over $S$ of pro-\'etale type,
% 11468: given by (5.5.8). Finally the groups $\Tl(A_K)^f$ and
% 11469: $\Tl(A_K)/\Tl(A_K)^t$ are the generic fibres of Barsotti--Tate groups
% 11470: defined over $S$, equal respectively to $\Tl(A^0)^f$ and to
% 11471: $D(\Tl(A'^0)^f) = \Hom(\Tl(A'^0)^f, \ZZ_{\ell}(1))$.
% 11472: \end{proposition}
% 11473: 
% 11474: \noindent\textbf{5.6.1.} One will note that it amounts to the same for the
% 11475: \'etale covering $S'$ to split $\Tl(A_K)^t$ or to split
% 11476: $\Tl(A_K)/\Tl(A_K)^f$, for this means respectively that $S'$ splits the
% 11477: twisted constant groups $\underline{M}$ and $\underline{M}'$, which are
% 11478: isogenous, since $T_0$ and $T'_0$ are so. One sees at the same time that
% 11479: there exists a smallest \'etale extension $S'$ of $S$ which does the job.
% 11480: 
% 11481: Let us also mention the following easy consequence of the definitions of
% 11482: 2.2.3 :
% 11483: 
% 11484: \begin{proposition}[5.7]
% 11485: Suppose $A^0$ is $\ell$-divisible, $S$ henselian. Let $F_K$ be a
% 11486: constructible $\ell$-adic sheaf on $K$ which is ``unramified'' i.e.
% 11487: which extends to a constant twisted $\ell$-adic sheaf $F$ on $S$, and
% 11488: let $u \colon F_K \to \Tl(A_K)$ be a homomorphism of pro-groups, then
% 11489: $u$ factorises into a homomorphism from $F$ into $\Tl(A_K)^f$.
% 11490: \end{proposition}
% 11491: 
% 11492: \begin{proof}
% 11493: Indeed, one will have
% 11494: \begin{equation}
% 11495: u = (u(\nu))_{\nu \geq 0} \; , \quad
% 11496: \text{with } u(\nu) \colon F_K(\nu) \longrightarrow
% 11497: \Tl(A_K)(\nu) = {}_{\ell^{\nu}}\!A_K \; .
% 11498: \end{equation}
% 11499: By the universal property of $A$, as $F(\nu)$ is \'etale hence smooth
% 11500: over $S$, the morphism $u(\nu)$ comes from a homomorphism
% 11501: \[
% 11502: v(\nu) \colon F(\nu) \longrightarrow {}_{\ell^{\nu}}\!A \; ,
% 11503: \]
% 11504: and by definition of the fixed part, this homomorphism factorises as
% 11505: \[
% 11506: v(\nu) \colon F(\nu) \longrightarrow ({}_{\ell^{\nu}}\!A)^f \; .
% 11507: \]
% 11508: As the image of the induced morphism on the special fibres falls into
% 11509: the infinitely $\ell$-divisible part of $A_0$, one concludes that
% 11510: $v(\nu)$ even factorises through $({}_{\ell^{\nu}}\!A^0)^f$. By passage
% 11511: to the limit on $\nu$, one finds a homomorphism
% 11512: \[
% 11513: v \colon F \longrightarrow \Tl(A^0)^f \; ,
% 11514: \]
% 11515: and the morphism induced on the generic fibres evidently gives the
% 11516: sought factorisation.
% 11517: \end{proof}
% 11518: 
% 11519: 
% 11520: \noindent\textbf{5.8.} Using the orthogonality theorem, we shall now
% 11521: obtain the main variants of the N\'eron--Ogg--Shafarevich criterion :
% 11522: 
% 11523: \begin{theoreme}[5.8]
% 11524: Let $A_K$ be an abelian scheme over $K$. Suppose that $S$ is strictly
% 11525: local, and let $\ell$ be a prime number $\neq p$. The following
% 11526: conditions are equivalent :
% 11527: \begin{enumerate}[label=\emph{(\roman*)}]
% 11528: \item $A_K$ has \emph{good reduction} over $S$, i.e. $A_K$ extends to an
% 11529: abelian scheme over $S$.
% 11530: \item The specialisation homomorphism $I \to \Aut(\Tl(A_K))$ is
% 11531: \emph{trivial}.
% 11532: \end{enumerate}
% 11533: \end{theoreme}
% 11534: 
% 11535: \begin{theoreme}[5.9]
% 11536: With the notation of \emph{5.8}, suppose $A_K$ has semi-stable
% 11537: reduction over $S$. Then the following equivalent conditions :
% 11538: \begin{enumerate}[label=\emph{(\roman*)}]
% 11539: \item $A_K$ has \emph{good reduction} over $S$.
% 11540: \item $\Tl(A_K)^t = 0$.
% 11541: \item $\Tl(A_K)^t = 0$ for \emph{one} prime number $\ell \neq p$.
% 11542: \end{enumerate}
% 11543: \end{theoreme}
% 11544: 
% 11545: \begin{theoreme}[5.10]
% 11546: With the notation of \emph{5.8} (but without necessarily supposing $A_K$
% 11547: to have semi-stable reduction), the following equivalent conditions :
% 11548: \begin{enumerate}[label=\emph{(\roman*)}]
% 11549: \item $A_K$ has \emph{good reduction} over $S$.
% 11550: \item $\Tl(A_K)^f = 0$ for \emph{one} prime number $\ell \neq p$.
% 11551: \item $\Tl(A_K)^f = 0$ for \emph{every} prime number $\ell \neq p$.
% 11552: \end{enumerate}
% 11553: \end{theoreme}
% 11554: 
% 11555: \begin{proof}
% 11556: (i) $\Rightarrow$ (ii) is clear, for if $A_K$ has good reduction, then
% 11557: $A^0 = A$ and $\Tl(A^0)^f = \Tl(A)$, hence $\Tl(A_K)^f = 0$.
% 11558: 
% 11559: (ii) $\Rightarrow$ (iii) is trivial.
% 11560: 
% 11561: (iii) $\Rightarrow$ (i) : If $\Tl(A_K)^f = 0$, then $A_0^0$ is
% 11562: unipotent (2.2.5). But for it to be zero, it is necessary and
% 11563: sufficient that the euclidean dual of $\Tl(A_K)/\Tl(A_K)^f$ be zero,
% 11564: which still means that the maximal torus of $A_0'^0$ is zero, i.e.
% 11565: $A_K'$ has good reduction. As $A_K'' = A_K$, this means that $A_K$ has
% 11566: good reduction.
% 11567: \end{proof}
% 11568: 
% 11569: \medskip
% 11570: 
% 11571: \noindent\textbf{5.11.} One also finds criteria for good reduction as
% 11572: well as for semi-stable reduction in terms of finite groups :
% 11573: 
% 11574: \begin{theoreme}[5.12]
% 11575: Suppose $S$ strictly local, and let $n$ be an integer $\geq 1$ prime to
% 11576: $p$. The following conditions are equivalent :
% 11577: \begin{enumerate}[label=\emph{(\roman*)}]
% 11578: \item $A_K$ has \emph{good reduction} over $S$.
% 11579: \item The group ${}_n A_K$ is unramified over $S$, i.e. $I$ acts
% 11580: trivially on ${}_n A(\overline{K})$.
% 11581: \end{enumerate}
% 11582: \end{theoreme}
% 11583: 
% 11584: \begin{proof}
% 11585: If $A_K$ has good reduction, ${}_n A$ is finite and smooth over $S$, hence
% 11586: ${}_n A_K$ is unramified. Conversely, suppose that ${}_n A_K$ is
% 11587: unramified. Then ${}_n A$ is a finite and flat scheme over $S$ (SGA 3 X
% 11588: 5.9), and as ${}_n A_K$ is unramified, the special fibre ${}_n A_0$ is
% 11589: \'etale over $k$. Hence ${}_n A$ is \'etale over $S$. For $p > 1$, this
% 11590: implies that $A_0$ is an abelian scheme, hence $A_K$ has good
% 11591: reduction.
% 11592: \end{proof}
% 11593: 
% 11594: \begin{theoreme}[5.13]
% 11595: With the notation of \emph{5.12} (but without supposing $A_K$ to have
% 11596: semi-stable reduction), the following equivalent conditions :
% 11597: \begin{enumerate}[label=\emph{(\roman*)}]
% 11598: \item $A_K$ has \emph{semi-stable reduction} over $S$.
% 11599: \item For every element $g \in I$, the multiplicity of $1$ as a root
% 11600: of the characteristic polynomial of the automorphism of
% 11601: $V_{\ell}(A_K)$ defined by $g$ is equal to $2\alpha$, where $\alpha$
% 11602: denotes the rank of the abelian scheme $B_0^{A_K}$ appearing in the
% 11603: decomposition (2.2.2) of the special fibre of the N\'eron model of
% 11604: $A_K$.
% 11605: \end{enumerate}
% 11606: \end{theoreme}
% 11607: 
% 11608: \begin{proof}
% 11609: (i) $\Rightarrow$ (ii) : If $A_K$ has semi-stable reduction, then the
% 11610: characteristic polynomial of $g \in I$ is of the form
% 11611: $(t-1)^{2\alpha} P(t)$, where $P(1) \neq 0$, whence (ii).
% 11612: 
% 11613: (ii) $\Rightarrow$ (i) : This is a criterion of M.~RAYNAUD, whose
% 11614: proof will be given elsewhere.
% 11615: \end{proof}
% 11616: 
% 11617: \begin{theoreme}[5.14]
% 11618: Suppose $S$ strictly local. Let $\ell$ be a prime number $\neq p$, $A_K$
% 11619: an abelian scheme over $K$, and suppose that $A_K$ has semi-stable
% 11620: reduction. Consider the canonical injection
% 11621: \begin{equation}
% 11622: \Tl(A_K)^f \lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow \Tl(A_K) \; ,
% 11623: \end{equation}
% 11624: and let $\mu$ be the rank of the toric factor of the special fibre of
% 11625: the N\'eron model of $A_K$. Then the following equivalent conditions :
% 11626: \begin{enumerate}[label=\emph{(\roman*)}]
% 11627: \item $\mu = 0$, i.e. $A_K$ has \emph{good reduction} over $S$.
% 11628: \item $I$ acts trivially on $\Tl(A_K)/\Tl(A_K)^f$, and the
% 11629: characteristic polynomial of the automorphism defined by an element $g
% 11630: \in I$ on $\Tl(A_K)/\Tl(A_K)^f$ has rational integer coefficients
% 11631: independent of $\ell$.
% 11632: \end{enumerate}
% 11633: \end{theoreme}
% 11634: 
% 11635: \begin{theoreme}[5.15]
% 11636: With the notation of \emph{5.14}, suppose $k$ finite. Then the
% 11637: following conditions are equivalent :
% 11638: \begin{enumerate}[label=\emph{(\roman*)}]
% 11639: \item $A_K$ has \emph{semi-stable reduction} over $S$.
% 11640: \item $\Tl(A_K)/\Tl(A_K)^f$ and $\Tl(A_K)^t$ are defined over $K$
% 11641: (i.e. $I$ acts trivially on these groups).
% 11642: \end{enumerate}
% 11643: \end{theoreme}
% 11644: 
% 11645: 
% 11646: \newpage
% 11647: \hrule
% 11648: \medskip
% 11649: 
% 11650: \noindent\textbf{\large \S\,6. Remarks on the construction of the toric
% 11651: and fixed parts in the non-semi-stable case.}
% 11652: 
% 11653: \smallskip
% 11654: 
% 11655: \noindent\textbf{6.1.} Let us now return to the general situation of
% 11656: \S\,2, that is, we do not suppose $A_K$ to have semi-stable reduction.
% 11657: One then has, for every prime number $\ell \neq p$, the filtrations
% 11658: (1.3.2) and (2.3.0) :
% 11659: \begin{equation}
% 11660: \Tl(A_K)^t \;\subset\; \Tl(A_K)^f \;\subset\; \Tl(A_K) \; .
% 11661: \end{equation}
% 11662: The fundamental result of 3.6 teaches us that the toric part
% 11663: $\Tl(A_K)^t$ is zero for $\ell$ sufficiently large, i.e. if $K'$ is a
% 11664: finite extension of $K$ such that $A_{K'}$ has semi-stable reduction
% 11665: over $S'$, then $I' = \Gal(\overline{K}/K')$ acts trivially on
% 11666: $\Tl(A_K)^t$, so that $\Tl(A_K)^t$ is the generic fibre of a
% 11667: Barsotti--Tate pro-group \emph{of toric type} over $S$. Likewise, the
% 11668: fixed part $\Tl(A_K)^f$ is defined for all $\ell \neq p$, and we have
% 11669: seen in 2.2 that $\Tl(A_K)^f$ is the generic fibre of a Barsotti--Tate
% 11670: pro-group over $S$, restriction to $\eta$ of $\Tl(A^0)^f$, when $S$ is
% 11671: strictly local and $A^0$ is $\ell$-divisible.
% 11672: 
% 11673: \smallskip
% 11674: 
% 11675: We now propose to show how the results of \S\,\S\,4, 5 allow in their
% 11676: turn to simplify and make precise the structure of the filtration
% 11677: (6.1.1) in the general case, and to construct the toric and fixed
% 11678: parts in an intrinsic way, without passing through the semi-stable
% 11679: reduction.
% 11680: 
% 11681: \medskip
% 11682: 
% 11683: \noindent\textbf{6.2.} Let $S$ be strictly local. Recall (1.4) that we
% 11684: have constructed a \emph{biextension} $W$ of $(A^0, A'^0)$ by
% 11685: $\GG_{mS}$, where $A'$ is the N\'eron model of the dual $A'_K$ of
% 11686: $A_K$. This biextension reduces on the special fibre to a biextension
% 11687: $W_0$ of $(A_0^0, A_0'^0)$ by $\GG_{mk}$, and this biextension defines a
% 11688: divisor correspondence, identifying $B'_0$ with the dual of $B_0$ (and
% 11689: reciprocally), where $B_0$ and $B'_0$ denote the ``abelian parts'' of
% 11690: $A_0^0$ and $A_0'^0$ respectively (2.2.2).
% 11691: 
% 11692: Let $T_0$ be the maximal torus of $A_0^0$, and $T'_0$ the maximal torus
% 11693: of $A_0'^0$. According to 2.2.6, the twisted constant group schemes
% 11694: $M = D(T'_0)$ and $M' = D(T_0)$ are isogenous. The fact that $W_0$
% 11695: vanishes on $T_0 \times T'_0$ (VIII 4.10) means that the pairing
% 11696: \begin{equation}
% 11697: \Tl(T_0) \times \Tl(T'_0) \longrightarrow \Tl(\GG_{mk})
% 11698: \end{equation}
% 11699: is trivial, which is evident since $\Tl(T_0)$ and $\Tl(T'_0)$ are
% 11700: Barsotti--Tate pro-groups of toric type, and every homomorphism from
% 11701: such a group to $\ZZ_{\ell}(1) = \Tl(\GG_{mS})$ is trivial. The
% 11702: vanishing of (6.2.1) allows one to pass to the quotient and obtain a
% 11703: pairing :
% 11704: \begin{equation}
% 11705: \Tl(B_0) \times \Tl(B'_0) \longrightarrow \Tl(\GG_{mk}) \; ,
% 11706: \end{equation}
% 11707: which is a \emph{perfect duality} according to 5.4.
% 11708: 
% 11709: \medskip
% 11710: 
% 11711: \noindent\textbf{6.3.} Let $\ell$ be a prime number $\neq p$. We shall
% 11712: now show that the factors of the filtration (6.1.1) can be
% 11713: reconstructed in terms of the filtration (1.3.2) and of the biextension
% 11714: pairing. Indeed, consider the exact sequence of Galois cohomology
% 11715: \begin{equation}
% 11716: 0 \longrightarrow \Tl(A_K)^t \longrightarrow \Tl(A_K)^f
% 11717: \longrightarrow \Tl(A_K)^{\ab} \longrightarrow 0 \; ,
% 11718: \end{equation}
% 11719: where $\Tl(A_K)^{\ab}$ denotes the quotient
% 11720: $\Tl(A_K)^f/\Tl(A_K)^t$, and the dual exact sequence
% 11721: \begin{equation}
% 11722: 0 \longrightarrow \Tl(A'_K)^t \longrightarrow \Tl(A'_K)^f
% 11723: \longrightarrow \Tl(A'_K)^{\ab} \longrightarrow 0 \; .
% 11724: \end{equation}
% 11725: The biextension pairing defines a perfect duality
% 11726: \begin{equation}
% 11727: \Tl(A_K)^{\ab} \times \Tl(A'_K)^{\ab} \longrightarrow
% 11728: \ZZ_{\ell}(1) \; ,
% 11729: \end{equation}
% 11730: so that $\Tl(A_K)^{\ab}$ and $\Tl(A'_K)^{\ab}$ determine one another by
% 11731: duality.
% 11732: 
% 11733: \medskip
% 11734: 
% 11735: \noindent\textbf{6.4.} We have seen in 2.2.6 that the ranks of the tori
% 11736: $T_0$ and $T'_0$ are equal. More precisely, the constant twisted groups
% 11737: $M = D(T'_0)$ and $M' = D(T_0)$ are isogenous. In fact, the divisor
% 11738: correspondence $W_0^{\ab}$ allows us to define a \emph{canonical
% 11739: isomorphism} between the $\QQ_{\ell}$-vector spaces
% 11740: \begin{equation}
% 11741: V_{\ell}(A_K)^t \otimes_{\ZZ_{\ell}} \QQ_{\ell} \simeq
% 11742: V_{\ell}(A'_K)^t \otimes_{\ZZ_{\ell}} \QQ_{\ell} \; ,
% 11743: \end{equation}
% 11744: and the structure of the factors of the filtration (6.1.1) is completely
% 11745: determined by the biextension pairing.
% 11746: 
% 11747: \medskip
% 11748: 
% 11749: \noindent\textbf{6.5.} To conclude, let us point out that the
% 11750: construction of the toric part in the case where $\ell = p$ (hence in
% 11751: residual characteristic $p$) is done by the same method as in 5.1 :
% 11752: one defines the toric part by
% 11753: \begin{equation}
% 11754: \Tp(A^0)^t = \Tp(\hat{T}) \subset \Tp(A^0)^f \; ,
% 11755: \end{equation}
% 11756: where $\hat{T}$ is the formal torus defined in (5.1.3). This
% 11757: construction is valid under the hypothesis that $A^0$ is
% 11758: $p$-divisible, which is the case when $A_K$ has semi-stable reduction
% 11759: over $S$ (3.5). In the general case, one can always define
% 11760: $\Tp(A^0)^t$ as the largest sub-pro-Barsotti--Tate group of toric type
% 11761: contained in $\Tp(A^0)^f$.
% 11762: 
% 11763: \medskip
% 11764: 
% 11765: \noindent\textbf{6.6.} Let us summarise the main properties of the
% 11766: monodromy filtration in the general case : to every abelian scheme
% 11767: $A_K$ over $K$, one has associated a canonical filtration :
% 11768: \begin{equation}
% 11769: \Tl(A_K)^t \;\subset\; \Tl(A_K)^f \;\subset\; \Tl(A_K) \; ,
% 11770: \quad (\ell \neq p) \; ,
% 11771: \end{equation}
% 11772: whose successive factors are :
% 11773: \begin{itemize}
% 11774: \item $\Tl(A_K)^t$ : toric part, of rank $\mu = \dim T_0$, defined
% 11775: over a finite extension of $K$ (semi-stable reduction) ;
% 11776: \item $\Tl(A_K)^{\ab} = \Tl(A_K)^f/\Tl(A_K)^t$ : abelian part, of
% 11777: rank $2\alpha = 2\dim B_0$, in perfect duality with the abelian part
% 11778: of the dual ;
% 11779: \item $\Tl(A_K)/\Tl(A_K)^f$ : \'etale part, of rank $\mu$, isomorphic
% 11780: to $M' \otimes \ZZ_{\ell}$ for the semi-stable reduction.
% 11781: \end{itemize}
% 11782: 
% 11783: \smallskip
% 11784: 
% 11785: The ranks of these groups are related to the invariants of the
% 11786: decomposition (2.2.2) by :
% 11787: \begin{equation}
% 11788: \dim A_K = \alpha + \lambda + \mu \; ,
% 11789: \end{equation}
% 11790: where $\alpha = \dim B_0$, $\lambda = \dim U_0$, $\mu = \dim T_0$.
% 11791: 
% 11792: In the semi-stable reduction case ($\lambda = 0$), one has
% 11793: $\dim A_K = \alpha + \mu$, and the monodromy filtration reduces to :
% 11794: \begin{equation}
% 11795: 0 \;\subset\; \Tl(A_K)^t \;\subset\; \Tl(A_K)^f \;\subset\;
% 11796: \Tl(A_K) \; ,
% 11797: \end{equation}
% 11798: with :
% 11799: \begin{itemize}
% 11800: \item $\rang \Tl(A_K)^t = \mu$,
% 11801: \item $\rang \Tl(A_K)^f = 2\alpha + \mu$,
% 11802: \item $\rang \Tl(A_K)/\Tl(A_K)^f = \mu$.
% 11803: \end{itemize}
% 11804: 
% 11805:
